# Universal optimality of the triangular lattice

Coordinate `EN-108-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0199` | `PM-090.2` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Universal-optimality-of-the-triangular-lattice-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Universal-optimality-of-the-triangular-lattice-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove universal energy minimality for the triangular lattice in the plane. Among locally finite configurations of centered density one, it minimizes the lower limit of centered-ball energy averages for every nonnegative completely monotone function of squared distance, including when the energy is infinite. We also prove triangular minimality for planar logarithmic and Riesz renormalized energies, with $`0\\lt s\\lt 2`$ in the Riesz case, and the corresponding jellium minima. The proof uses sharp Gaussian Fourier bounds, positive mixtures, and heat-kernel comparison.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove universal energy minimality for the triangular lattice in the plane. Among locally finite configurations of centered density one, it minimizes the lower limit of centered-ball energy averages for every nonnegative completely monotone function of squared distance, including when the energy is infinite. We also prove triangular minimality for planar logarithmic and Riesz renormalized energies, with $`0\lt s\lt 2`$ in the Riesz case, and the corresponding jellium minima. The proof uses sharp Gaussian Fourier bounds, positive mixtures, and heat-kernel comparison.

- **claim-universal-energy-minimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-universal)): Let $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth and completely monotone, meaning that $(-1)^r g^{(r)}(t)\ge0$ for every integer $r\ge0$ and every $t>0$. For every locally finite configuration $\mathcal C\subset\mathbb R^2$ of centered disk density one, the following equality and inequality hold in the extended nonnegative reals: $$\label{eq:main-inequality} E_g(\mathcal C)\ge \sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}g(\left\lvert a\right\rvert^2)=E_g(A).$$
  - Explanation: The triangular lattice of covolume one attains the smallest lower-limit centered-ball energy among all locally finite configurations of centered disk density one, for every smooth completely monotone interaction of squared distance, with infinite values allowed.
- **claim-normalized-gaussian-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-normalized-pair)): For every finite real $k\ge2.36$, put $T_k(s)=k^{-1}e^{-k(s-1)}$. There are entire functions $H_1,H_2$, real on the real axis, and a real radial Schwartz function $f$ on $\mathbb R^2$ such that $$f(x)=e^{-\pi h b\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2}H_1(b\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2),\qquad \widehat f(\xi)=e^{-\pi h b\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^2}H_2(b\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^2).$$ For every real $s\ge0$, $$\label{eq:normalized-signs} H_1(s)\le T_k(s),\qquad H_2(s)\ge0.$$ At every $n\in\mathcal N$, the values and first derivatives satisfy $$\label{eq:normalized-jets} \begin{gathered} H_1(n)=T_k(n),\qquad H_1'(n)=T_k'(n)=-e^{-k(n-1)},\\ H_2(n)=H_2'(n)=0. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: For each admissible parameter there is a radial Schwartz function and its Fourier transform, built from entire profiles, which obey the Gaussian sign inequalities and match values and first derivatives of the target at every positive node.
- **lem-cardinal-measure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cardinal-measure)): The function in <a href="#eq:cardinal-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cardinal-function">[eq:cardinal-function]</a> extends to an entire function, real on the real axis, with jets $$\label{eq:cardinal-jets} p_a(n)=Q_nc_n,\qquad p_a'(n)=Q_n(d_n+D_nc_n) \quad(n\in\mathcal N_0).$$ There is a finite complex measure $\mu_a$ on $[-1,1]$ such that $$\label{eq:full-measure} p_a(s)=\int_{-1}^{1}e^{i\pi ts}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\mu_a(t)\qquad(s\in\mathbb C),$$ $\mu_a(-E)=\overline{\mu_a(E)}$ for every Borel set $E$, and $$\label{eq:spectral-variation} \\|\mu_a\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le 25\|C\|+36\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_0}(\|c_n\|+\|d_n\|).$$ For real $s$, the same value can be evaluated from a folded measure on $[0,1]$: $$\label{eq:folded-evaluation} p_a(s)=\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\int_0^1e^{i\pi ts}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\nu_a(t).$$ The atoms of $\nu_a$ are $Ce_jP_j$ at $t_j$, where $e_0=1$ and $e_j=2$ for $1\le j\le6$. Its density on the open frequency intervals is $$\label{eq:folded-density} d_j(t)=2\pi\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_0}e^{-i\pi tn} \sum_{l=j}^{6} \bigl[-\pi c_n(t_l-t)+id_n\bigr]P_le^{i\pi t_ln} \quad(t_{j-1}<t<t_j).$$ The same variation bound holds for $\nu_a$.
  - Explanation: A summable coefficient list defines an entire cardinal function with prescribed node jets, represented by a finite spectral measure on a compact frequency interval with a variation bound and a folded real-axis evaluation.
- **remark-frequency-consistency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The rational formulas in <a href="#eq:transformed-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transformed-kernel">[eq:transformed-kernel]</a> also make sense for complex $t\ne-ih$. Since $z(t)+ih=-B/(t+ih)$, the image avoids $-ih$, and substitution twice gives $$z(z(t))=t,\qquad \lambda(t)\lambda(z(t)) =\frac{i}{b(t+ih)}\frac{-i(t+ih)}{bB}=1.$$ These are pointwise frequency and amplitude identities for the Gaussian transform. They do not define a composition of the folded real-contour integral: $z$ does not preserve that contour, and a transformed profile has not thereby been given a cardinal measure on $[-1,1]$. The involution for the radial functions follows from the genuine Fourier transform below.
  - Explanation: The rational frequency map is an involution with a reciprocal amplitude identity, but this is only a pointwise identity and does not compose the real-contour spectral integral.
- **lem-fourier-pair** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-pair)): For every coefficient list $a$, the radial functions $e^{-\pi hs}p_a(s)$ and $e^{-\pi hs}K_{p_a}(s)$ are real Schwartz functions on $\mathbb R^2$ and are Fourier transforms of one another. Consequently, for any two lists, the entire functions $$\label{eq:coupled-pair} H_1=p_1+K_{p_2},\qquad H_2=p_2+K_{p_1}$$ give the real radial Schwartz Fourier pair $$f(x)=e^{-\pi hb\|x\|^2}H_1(b\|x\|^2),\qquad \widehat f(\xi)=e^{-\pi hb\|\xi\|^2}H_2(b\|\xi\|^2).$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian damping turns the direct cardinal function and the transformed kernel into real radial Schwartz functions that are Fourier transforms of each other, so two lists give a real radial Fourier pair.
- **lem-kernel-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-bounds)): For $0\le t\le1$, $$\label{eq:global-kernel-bounds} \|\lambda(t)\|<3,\qquad \|z(t)\|\le\frac{44}{15}<3, \qquad \pi\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z(t)>.187.$$ The last quantity exceeds $.70$ when $0\le t\le5/6$. On $5/6\le t\le1$, the sharper bounds are $$\label{eq:last-interval-bounds} \|\lambda(t)\|<1.25,\qquad \|z(t)\|<1.32, \qquad \pi\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z(t)>.187+2.48(1-t).$$ The operator $S$, with extra input coordinates included if desired, maps coefficient lists boundedly to positive-node $\ell^1$ lists and has norm less than 4000. More precisely, if $$w\ge\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_0}(\|c_n\|+\|d_n\|),\qquad c\ge\|C\|,$$ then for every positive integer $r$, $$\label{eq:tail-bound} \sum_{\substack{n\in\mathcal N\\n\ge r}} \bigl(\|(Sa)_{c,n}\|+\|(Sa)_{d,n}\|\bigr) \le U_r(w,c):= 800(w+c)\frac{e^{-.70r}}{1-e^{-.70}} +5.3\left(\frac{2.54w}{r}+2c\right) \frac{e^{-.187r}}{1-e^{-.187}}.$$ For several lists, the same formula bounds the sum of their tail outputs when $w,c$ bound the corresponding sums of input coefficients.
  - Explanation: Explicit bounds on the transformed kernel make the jet operator bounded on summable lists, with exponentially small outputs at distant nodes, uniformly in where the input coefficients sit.
- **lem-quadrature-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-quadrature-error)): Let $p$ and $K_p$ be the exact direct and transformed functions for one basis input among the $171$ coordinates $(\mathcal F,e)$. For every integer $a\ge0$, real $0\le m\le168$, and real $\|y\|\le3/2$, the replacement <a href="#eq:quadrature-rule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quadrature-rule">[eq:quadrature-rule]</a>, with all atoms kept exactly, changes each of $$\frac{y^a}{a!}p^{(a)}(m),\qquad \frac{y^a}{a!}K_p^{(a)}(m)$$ by less than $10^{-31}$ in absolute value.
  - Explanation: Replacing the six density integrals by the positive Fejer-type rule, with atoms kept exact, changes every scaled moment of a basis input by a tiny uniform amount.
- **lem-parameter-box** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-box)): Let $K,J$ be consecutive endpoints in the displayed list. For every real row $l$ as above and every finite $k$ with $K\le k\le J$, where the upper inequality is omitted if $J=\infty$, one has $lq(k)\ge[l]_{K,J}$.
  - Explanation: A linear form in the target data vector along the one-parameter curve is bounded below by the minimum over the vertices of a containing box.
- **prop-finite-certificate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-certificate)): The exact arrays defined in this section satisfy $$\label{eq:finite-norms} \begin{gathered} \left\lVert D^{64}\right\rVert<.004,\qquad \left\lVert V_+\right\rVert+\left\lVert V_-\right\rVert<36,\\ \sum_{i=1}^2\left\lVert(X_i)_j\right\rVert_1< \begin{cases}2.3,&1\le j\le5,\\5.6,&6\le j\le10.\end{cases} \end{gathered}$$ For every specified half-gap, $i=1,2$, consecutive parameter endpoints $K,J$, and $0\le d\le40$, $$\label{eq:bernstein-certificate} [L_{i,d}]_{K,J} +\mathbf1_{i=1}\sum_{r=0}^d\omega_{dr}a_r^*>.001.$$ For every box and $i=1,2$, $$\label{eq:tail-certificate} \begin{split} [C_i^*]_{K,J} &+\frac{\eta_{K,J}\bigl(\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_{\le88}}d^*_{i,n}\bigr)}{s_0}\\ &+\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_{\le88}}\left( \frac{\eta_{K,J}(u_{i,n})}{(s_0-n)^2} +\frac{n\,\eta_{K,J}(d^*_{i,n})}{s_0(s_0-n)}\right)>.0021. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The explicit finite arrays satisfy strict norm bounds for matrix powers, geometric sums and reference columns, together with positive lower margins for every Bernstein coefficient on each half-gap and for the tail expression.
- **prop-exact-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-interpolation)): For every real $k\ge2.36$, there is a unique pair of positive-node lists in $\mathcal E$, with extra coordinates $e_i=E_iq(k)$, whose full lists $\ell_i$ give $$\begin{aligned} H_1(n)&=T_k(n),& H_1'(n)&=T_k'(n),\\ H_2(n)&=0,& H_2'(n)&=0 \end{aligned} \qquad(n\in\mathcal N), \label{eq:exact-jets}$$ where $H_1=p_{\ell_1}+K_{p_{\ell_2}}$ and $H_2=p_{\ell_2}+K_{p_{\ell_1}}$. Let $\ell_i^{\mathrm{ref}}=X_iq(k)$, extended by zero on $\mathcal T$. Then $$\sum_{i=1}^2\left\lVert\ell_i^{\mathrm{ref}}\right\rVert_1<4.8,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^2\left\lVert\ell_i-\ell_i^{\mathrm{ref}}\right\rVert_1<2\cdot10^{-8},\qquad \sum_{i=1}^2\left\lVert\ell_i\right\rVert_1<5. \label{eq:exact-list-bounds}$$ The functions $H_1,H_2$ are entire and real on the real axis. In particular, $H_1-T_k$ and $H_2$ have zeros of order at least two at every positive node, including the nodes that are not lattice radii. Their Gaussian-damped functions form the real radial Schwartz Fourier pair of Lemma <a href="#lem:fourier-pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fourier-pair">5</a>.
  - Explanation: For each admissible parameter the coupled jet equations have a unique solution in the summable coefficient system with the prescribed extras, close to the finite reference lists and giving genuine double zeros at all positive nodes.
- **lem-exponential-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exponential-support)): Let $K,J$ be consecutive endpoints of the parameter partition in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">3</a>, and let $K\le k\le J$, with no upper restriction when $J=\infty$. On every prescribed half-gap, the coefficients $a_r^*$ from <a href="#eq:target-support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-support">[eq:target-support]</a> satisfy $$\mathcal D_{m,\nu}T_k(m+yx) \ge\sum_{r=0}^{40}a_r^*x^r-.000001\qquad(0\le x\le1).$$
  - Explanation: On each half-gap and parameter interval the target quotient is bounded below by an explicit degree-forty polynomial minus a tiny truncation allowance.
- **lem-half-gap-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-half-gap-signs)): For every real $k\ge2.36$, the exact slacks satisfy $F_i(s)\ge0$ for $0\le s\le s_0$, $i=1,2$.
  - Explanation: On the bounded radial range both exact slacks are nonnegative, by combining the Bernstein margin with the list, truncation and target-support error allowances.
- **lem-sine-product-barrier** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sine-product-barrier)): For every real $s$, let $m\in\mathcal Z$ be any nearest point of the full periodic zero set, so $\|s-m\|=\min_{n\in\mathcal Z}\|s-n\|$. Then $$\label{eq:P-quadratic-lower} P(s)\ge .68(s-m)^2.$$
  - Explanation: The periodic sine product is bounded below by a fixed multiple of the squared distance to the nearest zero.
- **lem-tail-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-signs)): For every real $k\ge2.36$, the exact slacks satisfy $F_i(s)\ge0$ for $s\ge s_0$, $i=1,2$.
  - Explanation: In the unbounded tail both exact slacks are nonnegative because the positive low-node rational part dominates a remainder with double zeros and a small second derivative.
- **prop-density-lp** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-density-lp)): Let $f$ be a real even Schwartz function on $\mathbb R^2$ whose Fourier transform is nonnegative. Every locally finite configuration $\mathcal C\subset\mathbb R^2$ with centered disk density one satisfies $$\label{eq:density-lp} \liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac1{N_R} \sum_{\substack{x,y\in\mathcal C_R\\x\ne y}}f(x-y) \ge \widehat f(0)-f(0).$$ The same lower bound holds if $f(x-y)$ is replaced by $\Phi(x-y)$, where $\Phi:\mathbb R^2\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty)$ satisfies $\Phi(z)\ge f(z)$.
  - Explanation: A real even Schwartz function with nonnegative Fourier transform bounds the lower-limit pair energy of any centered-density-one configuration from below, using only the density hypothesis and Cauchy-Schwarz.
- **lem-gaussian-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-duality)): For every $\alpha>0$, let $G_\alpha(x)=e^{-\pi\alpha\|x\|^2}$. There is a real radial Schwartz function $f_\alpha$ such that, pointwise, $$\label{eq:gaussian-sharp-pair} \begin{gathered} f_\alpha\le G_\alpha,\qquad \widehat f_\alpha\ge0,\\ f_\alpha(a)=G_\alpha(a)\quad(a\in A\setminus\{0\}),\qquad \widehat f_\alpha(w)=0\quad(w\in A^*\setminus\{0\}). \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Every Gaussian has a sharp radial Schwartz minorant with nonnegative Fourier transform and exact contacts on the direct and dual lattice shells, via scaling for large parameters and Fourier complementation for small ones.
- **lem-positive-mixture** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-mixture)): Let $F:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth, with one-sided derivatives at zero, and suppose $(-1)^jF^{(j)}(t)\ge0$ for every integer $j\ge0$ and every $t\ge0$. There is a finite positive Borel measure $\rho$ on $[0,1]$ of total mass $F(0)$ such that $$\label{eq:positive-mixture} F(t)=\int_{[0,1]}v^t\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\rho(v)\qquad(t>0).$$ Moreover, $\rho(\{0\})=0$. An atom at $1$ is allowed and contributes a constant function.
  - Explanation: A smooth completely monotone function on the closed half-line is a positive mixture of exponential kernels over the unit interval, with total mass equal to its value at zero and no atom at zero.
- **prop-energy-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-energy-reduction)): Let $\mathcal C\subset\mathbb R^2$ be locally finite with centered disk density one. Suppose that, for every $\alpha>0$, $$\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac1{N_R} \sum_{\substack{x,y\in\mathcal C_R\\x\ne y}}e^{-\pi\alpha\|x-y\|^2} \ge\sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}e^{-\pi\alpha\|a\|^2}.$$ Then every smooth completely monotone $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:full-energy-bound} E_g(\mathcal C)\ge\sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}g(\|a\|^2)$$ in the extended nonnegative reals, with the ordered-pair energy defined in <a href="#eq:energy-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-definition">[eq:energy-definition]</a>.
  - Explanation: A Gaussian lower bound for all parameters transfers to every smooth completely monotone interaction, using shifted mixtures, Fatou and Tonelli, and removing the shift only in the lattice sum.
- **cor-riesz-jellium** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-riesz-jellium)): Let $0\le s<2$. For every positive integer $n$, put $N=n^2$ and enumerate $A/(nA)$ by $a_1^\triangle,\ldots,a_N^\triangle$. Every configuration of $N$ distinct points in $\mathbb R^2/(nA)$ satisfies $$W_{s,nA}(a_1,\ldots,a_N) \ge W_{s,nA}(a_1^\triangle,\ldots,a_N^\triangle).$$ Moreover, $A$ minimizes $W_s$ among density-one planar configurations, and $e_{\mathrm{Jel}}^{(s)}=\kappa_s^{-1}W_s(A)$.
  - Explanation: The triangular lattice minimizes the renormalized energy on compatible tori and among density-one planar configurations, and the thermodynamic jellium minimum is determined by its value.
- **lem-periodic-heat-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison)): Let $0\le s<2$, and let $\mathcal C$ and $\mathcal D$ be simple periodic configurations of density one. Put $$q_0=2\pi,\qquad q_s=\frac{2^{2-s}\pi\Gamma(1-s/2)}{\Gamma(s/2)}\quad(0<s<2), \qquad \Psi_t(x)=(4\pi t)^{-1}e^{-\|x\|^2/(4t)}.$$ If $\mathcal C=\bigcup_{i=1}^N(a_i+L)$, where $\|\mathbb R^2/L\|=N$, define $$P_{t,L}(x)=\sum_{v\in L}\Psi_t(x-v),\qquad H_{\mathcal C}(t)=\frac1N\sum_{i,j=1}^N P_{t,L}(a_i-a_j)-\Psi_t(0),$$ and define $H_{\mathcal D}$ using its own period lattice and point count. Then the following integral is absolutely convergent and satisfies $$V_s(\mathcal C)-V_s(\mathcal D) =\frac{\kappa_s q_s}{\Gamma(1-s/2)}\int_0^\infty \bigl(H_{\mathcal C}(t)-H_{\mathcal D}(t)\bigr)t^{-s/2}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dt.$$
  - Explanation: The difference of canonical periodic energies of two simple density-one periodic configurations equals an absolutely convergent weighted integral of the difference of their heat-kernel profiles, even with different period lattices.
- **lem-rational-pi-b** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-pi-b)): For $b=\sqrt3/2$, these rationals satisfy $$\label{eq:rational-pi-b-error} p_*<\pi<p_*+\epsilon,\qquad b_*<b<b_*+\epsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Two explicit decimal rationals enclose the circle constant and the lattice constant within a tiny tolerance.
- **prop-normalized-cubic-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-normalized-cubic-approximation)): Let $0\le s<2$, and fix $1<p<2$ for $s=0$, or $1<p<\min(2,2/s,3/(s+1))$ for $s>0$. Let $\mathcal A_1$ be the compatible gradient fields in this fixed $L^p_{\rm loc}$ class, with simple locally finite configurations and unit background. Then the limit $$\mathcal W(E)=\lim_{\eta\downarrow0} \left[\limsup_{R\to\infty}R^{-2}Q_\eta(E,R) -\kappa g(\eta)\right]$$ exists in $\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$ and is bounded below uniformly over $\mathcal A_1$. Its infimum $m$ is finite. There are integers $R_j\to\infty$ and simple configurations $\mathcal C_j$, periodic under $2R_j\mathbb Z^2$, with $4R_j^2$ points per period cell, whose canonical periodic values satisfy $$V(\mathcal C_j)\longrightarrow m.$$ The approximating fields may therefore be chosen to be canonical periodic gradients in the same fixed-$p$ class.
  - Explanation: The field-energy infimum is finite and is approached by canonical periodic gradient fields on square tori in the fixed integrability class.
- **lem-field-cutoff-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-field-cutoff-control)): For compatible gradients in the prescribed fixed-$p$ class, allowing integer multiplicities, the cutoff limit $\mathcal W(E)$ exists in $\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$. There is a constant $C_s$ independent of the field such that $$-C_s\le\mathcal W_\eta(E)\le\mathcal W(E)+2\kappa F_\eta \qquad(0<\eta<\eta_0).$$ If $Q_\eta(E,R)=O(R^2)$ at one fixed cutoff, $Q_\rho(E,R)=O(R^2)$ holds at every fixed cutoff $\rho$ and $\nu(K_R)/R^2\to1$. In particular, the infimum $m$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:normalized-cubic-approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:normalized-cubic-approximation">22</a> is a finite real number.
  - Explanation: The cutoff limit of the renormalized energy exists, cutoff energies are uniformly bounded below and almost monotone, and volume-order energy at one cutoff gives unit density and a finite infimum.
- **lem-stationary-field-minimizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stationary-field-minimizers)): There is a horizontally stationary probability law $P$ on compatible simple gradient fields in the fixed-$p$ class, supported on $\mathcal W(E)=m$. For the selected dyadic cutoffs, put $$a_\eta=\mathbb E_P\mathcal W_\eta(E),\qquad D_\eta=\mathbb E_P\mu_\eta(Q).$$ Then $a_\eta\to m$ and $0\le D_\eta\le m-a_\eta+2\kappa F_\eta\to0$. For each selected $\eta$, one can choose a realization for which the spatial averages of every selected cutoff energy have finite limits, the density $d_\eta=\lim_{R\to\infty}R^{-2}\mu_\eta(K_R)$ satisfies $d_\eta\le2D_\eta$, and, when $k=1$, for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, $$\frac1{\varepsilon^4R^2} \int_{K_R\times\{\|y\|>\varepsilon^2R/4\}}w\|E\|^2\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: There is a horizontally stationary law on simple minimizing fields whose expected close-pair defect vanishes as the cutoff is removed, with a good realization selectable at each cutoff.
- **lem-field-screening** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-field-screening)): Fix the auxiliary cutoff $\eta'$ chosen above and $0<\eta<\eta'$. Let $E$ be a compatible simple gradient field for which $$M_{R,\rho}:=R^{-2}Q_\rho(E,R)$$ has a finite limit for $\rho=\eta,\eta'$. If $k=1$, suppose also that $e_{\varepsilon,R}$ defined in <a href="#eq:normalized-moving-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-moving-tail">[eq:normalized-moving-tail]</a> tends to zero for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$; set $e_{\varepsilon,R}=0$ if $k=0$. For every $0<\varepsilon<1/8$ and every sufficiently large integer $R$, there are exactly $R^2$ distinct points $\widehat\Lambda\subset K_R$ and a field $\widehat E$ with zero exterior normal flux satisfying $$-\operatorname{div}(w\widehat E) =\kappa\left(\sum_{p\in\widehat\Lambda}\delta_{(p,0)} -\mathbf1_{K_R}\delta_{\mathbb R^2}\right).$$ Its truncation has finite weighted energy and obeys $$\begin{aligned} R^{-2}\int_{K_R\times\mathbb R^k}w\|\widehat E_\eta\|^2 &\le M_{R,\eta} +C_{s,\eta'}\varepsilon(1+g(\eta)) (1+M_{R,\eta}+M_{R,\eta'})+o_R(1)\\ &\quad+C_s e_{\varepsilon,R} \varepsilon^{1+\min(2\gamma,0)}. \end{aligned}$$ Every pair in $\widehat\Lambda$ at distance below $2\eta$ is a retained pair of the original configuration in $K_R$, and every point has distance at least $a_0/2$ from $\partial K_R$. The field $\widehat E$ need not be a gradient. All constants are independent of $R$ and the realization; $R$ is chosen after $\eta',\eta$, the field, and $\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: A shell construction produces a screened field with zero exterior flux and the right number of distinct points, with controlled cutoff energy and no new close pairs.
- **lem-rational-roundoff** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-roundoff)): The floor prescription below has the following properties.* 1. *If $\|w\|<1700$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}w<7$, then $\|e_*(w)-e^w\|<10^{-68}$. Every exponential argument in the prescription lies in this domain.* 2. *A computed single-column quadrature moment of order at most $42$, scaled by $y^a/a!$ with $\|y\|\le3/2$, differs from the exact quadrature moment by less than $10^{-48}$. The initial operator-norm errors in $S^\#_{\mathcal F\mathcal F}$, $S^\#_{\mathcal F e}$, and $Y^\#$ are less than $10^{-44}$.* 3. *Suppose that either the exact or the rounded computation satisfies the following envelopes: every pre-update power in the nine steps has norm below $3$, each intermediate partial-sum matrix has norm below $34$, and every combined pair of corresponding $X_1,X_2$ columns has norm below $5.6$. Then the combined error in a pair of corresponding columns of $X_1^\#,X_2^\#$ is less than $10^{-29}$. Every entry of the Bernstein rows $L_{i,d}^\#$ differs from the corresponding entry of $L_{i,d}$ in <a href="#eq:bernstein-rows" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bernstein-rows">[eq:bernstein-rows]</a> by less than $10^{-16}$. Every lower evaluation in <a href="#eq:bernstein-certificate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bernstein-certificate">[eq:bernstein-certificate]</a> and every tail expression in <a href="#eq:tail-certificate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tail-certificate">[eq:tail-certificate]</a> has error less than $10^{-6}$.* *The stagewise envelopes in the last item follow from the stronger power and column tables, whether those tests are established on the exact arrays by intervals or on the rounded arrays by the prescription below.
  - Explanation: The floor-rounded rational prescription approximates the exponential, the moments, the matrix recurrences and the Bernstein and tail evaluations within explicit error ceilings, which transfers its tests to the looser exact-array bounds.

```json
        "exact_quote": "The exact arrays defined in this section satisfy $$\\label{eq:finite-norms}\n\\begin{gathered}\n \\left\\lVert D^{64}\\right\\rVert<.004,\\qquad \\left\\lVert V_+\\right\\rVert+\\left\\lVert V_-\\right\\rVert<36,\\\\\n \\sum_{i=1}^2\\left\\lVert(X_i)_j\\right\\rVert_1<\n \\begin{cases}2.3,&1\\le j\\le5,\\\\5.6,&6\\le j\\le10.\\end{cases}\n\\end{gathered}$$ For every specified half-gap, $i=1,2$, consecutive parameter endpoints $K,J$, and $0\\le d\\le40$, $$\\label{eq:bernstein-certificate}\n [L_{i,d}]_{K,J}\n +\\mathbf1_{i=1}\\sum_{r=0}^d\\omega_{dr}a_r^*>.001.$$ For every box and $i=1,2$, $$\\label{eq:tail-certificate}\n\\begin{split}\n [C_i^*]_{K,J}\n &+\\frac{\\eta_{K,J}\\bigl(\\sum_{n\\in\\mathcal N_{\\le88}}d^*_{i,n}\\bigr)}{s_0}\\\\\n &+\\sum_{n\\in\\mathcal N_{\\le88}}\\left(\n \\frac{\\eta_{K,J}(u_{i,n})}{(s_0-n)^2}\n +\\frac{n\\,\\eta_{K,J}(d^*_{i,n})}{s_0(s_0-n)}\\right)>.0021.\n\\end{split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "For every real $k\\ge2.36$, there is a unique pair of positive-node lists in $\\mathcal E$, with extra coordinates $e_i=E_iq(k)$, whose full lists $\\ell_i$ give $$\\begin{aligned}\n H_1(n)&=T_k(n),& H_1'(n)&=T_k'(n),\\\\\n H_2(n)&=0,& H_2'(n)&=0\n \\end{aligned}\n \\qquad(n\\in\\mathcal N),\n \\label{eq:exact-jets}$$ where $H_1=p_{\\ell_1}+K_{p_{\\ell_2}}$ and $H_2=p_{\\ell_2}+K_{p_{\\ell_1}}$. Let $\\ell_i^{\\mathrm{ref}}=X_iq(k)$, extended by zero on $\\mathcal T$. Then $$\\sum_{i=1}^2\\left\\lVert\\ell_i^{\\mathrm{ref}}\\right\\rVert_1<4.8,\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^2\\left\\lVert\\ell_i-\\ell_i^{\\mathrm{ref}}\\right\\rVert_1<2\\cdot10^{-8},\\qquad\n \\sum_{i=1}^2\\left\\lVert\\ell_i\\right\\rVert_1<5.\n \\label{eq:exact-list-bounds}$$ The functions $H_1,H_2$ are entire and real on the real axis. In particular, $H_1-T_k$ and $H_2$ have zeros of order at least two at every positive node, including the nodes that are not lattice radii. Their Gaussian-damped functions form the real radial Schwartz Fourier pair of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-pair\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-pair\">5</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-lp",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a real even Schwartz function on $\\mathbb R^2$ whose Fourier transform is nonnegative.         "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-lp",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a real even Schwartz function on $\\mathbb R^2$ whose Fourier transform is nonnegative. Every locally finite configuration $\\mathcal C\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ with centered disk density one satisfies $$\\label{eq:density-lp}\n \\liminf_{R\\to\\infty}\\frac1{N_R}\n \\sum_{\\substack{x,y\\in\\mathcal C_R\\\\x\\ne y}}f(x-y)\n \\ge \\widehat f(0)-f(0).$$ The same lower bound holds if $f(x-y)$ is replaced by $\\Phi(x-y)$, where $\\Phi:\\mathbb R^2\\setminus\\{0\\}\\to[0,\\infty)$ satisfies $\\Phi(z)\\ge f(z)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be smooth, with one-sided derivatives at zero, and suppose $(-1)^jF^{(j)}(t)\\ge0$ for every integer $j\\ge0$ and every $t\\ge0$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal C\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be locally finite with centered disk density one.         "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le s<2$. For every positive integer $n$, put $N=n^2$ and enumerate $A/(nA)$ by $a_1^\\triangle,\\ldots,a_N^\\triangle$.         "source_id": "PM-090.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le s<2$, and let $\\mathcal C$ and $\\mathcal D$ be simple periodic configurations of density one. Put $$q_0=2\\pi,\\qquad\n q_s=\\frac{2^{2-s}\\pi\\Gamma(1-s/2)}{\\Gamma(s/2)}\\quad(0<s<2),\n \\qquad \\Psi_t(x)=(4\\pi t)^{-1}e^{-|x|^2/(4t)}.$$ If $\\mathcal C=\\bigcup_{i=1}^N(a_i+L)$, where $|\\mathbb R^2/L|=N$, define $$P_{t,L}(x)=\\sum_{v\\in L}\\Psi_t(x-v),\\qquad\n H_{\\mathcal C}(t)=\\frac1N\\sum_{i,j=1}^N P_{t,L}(a_i-a_j)-\\Psi_t(0),$$ and define $H_{\\mathcal D}$ using its own period lattice and point count. Then the following integral is absolutely convergent and satisfies $$V_s(\\mathcal C)-V_s(\\mathcal D)\n =\\frac{\\kappa_s q_s}{\\Gamma(1-s/2)}\\int_0^\\infty\n       \\bigl(H_{\\mathcal C}(t)-H_{\\mathcal D}(t)\\bigr)t^{-s/2}\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dt.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le s<2$, and let $\\mathcal C$ and $\\mathcal D$ be simple periodic configurations of density one. Then the following integral is absolutely convergent and satisfies $$V_s(\\mathcal C)-V_s(\\mathcal D)\n =\\frac{\\kappa_s q_s}{\\Gamma(1-s/2)}\\int_0^\\infty\n       \\bigl(H_{\\mathcal C}(t)-H_{\\mathcal D}(t)\\bigr)t^{-s/2}\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dt.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $0\\le s<2$, and let $\\mathcal C$ and $\\mathcal D$ be simple periodic configurations of density one. Then the following integral is absolutely convergent and satisfies $$V_s(\\mathcal C)-V_s(\\mathcal D)\n =\\frac{\\kappa_s q_s}{\\Gamma(1-s/2)}\\int_0^\\infty\n       \\bigl(H_{\\mathcal C}(t)-H_{\\mathcal D}(t)\\bigr)t^{-s/2}\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm dt.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalized-cubic-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le s<2$, and fix $1<p<2$ for $s=0$, or $1<p<\\min(2,2/s,3/(s+1))$ for $s>0$. Let $\\mathcal A_1$ be the compatible gradient fields in this fixed $L^p_{\\rm loc}$ class, with simple locally finite configurations and unit background. Then the limit $$\\mathcal W(E)=\\lim_{\\eta\\downarrow0}\n \\left[\\limsup_{R\\to\\infty}R^{-2}Q_\\eta(E,R)\n                    -\\kappa g(\\eta)\\right]$$ exists in $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{+\\infty\\}$ and is bounded below uniformly over $\\mathcal A_1$. Its infimum $m$ is finite. There are integers $R_j\\to\\infty$ and simple configurations $\\mathcal C_j$, periodic under $2R_j\\mathbb Z^2$, with $4R_j^2$ points per period cell, whose canonical periodic values satisfy $$V(\\mathcal C_j)\\longrightarrow m.$$ The approximating fields may therefore be chosen to be canonical periodic gradients in the same fixed-$p$ class.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-field-cutoff-control",
        "exact_quote": "For compatible gradients in the prescribed fixed-$p$ class, allowing integer multiplicities, the cutoff limit $\\mathcal W(E)$ exists in $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{+\\infty\\}$. There is a constant $C_s$ independent of the field such that $$-C_s\\le\\mathcal W_\\eta(E)\\le\\mathcal W(E)+2\\kappa F_\\eta\n \\qquad(0<\\eta<\\eta_0).$$ If $Q_\\eta(E,R)=O(R^2)$ at one fixed cutoff, $Q_\\rho(E,R)=O(R^2)$ holds at every fixed cutoff $\\rho$ and $\\nu(K_R)/R^2\\to1$. In particular, the infimum $m$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:normalized-cubic-approximation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:normalized-cubic-approximation\">22</a> is a finite real number.",
        "source_id": "PM-090.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-field-cutoff-control",
          "exact_quote": "For compatible gradients in the prescribed fixed-$p$ class, allowing integer multiplicities, the cutoff limit $\\mathcal W(E)$ exists in $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{+\\infty\\}$. There is a constant $C_s$ independent of the field such that $$-C_s\\le\\mathcal W_\\eta(E)\\le\\mathcal W(E)+2\\kappa F_\\eta\n \\qquad(0<\\eta<\\eta_0).$$ If $Q_\\eta(E,R)=O(R^2)$ at one fixed cutoff, $Q_\\rho(E,R)=O(R^2)$ holds at every fixed cutoff $\\rho$ and $\\nu(K_R)/R^2\\to1$. In particular, the infimum $m$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:normalized-cubic-approximation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:normalized-cubic-approximation\">22</a> is a finite real number.",
          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For compatible gradients in the prescribed fixed-$p$ class, allowing integer multiplicities, the cutoff limit $\\mathcal W(E)$ exists in $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{+\\infty\\}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **triangular lattice A** ([source](#eq:energy-definition)): Put $b=\sqrt3/2$ and let $$A=b^{-1/2}\{j(1,0)+k(1/2,b):j,k\in\mathbb Z\}$$ be the triangular lattice of covolume one.
- **centered disk density one** ([source](#eq:energy-definition)): Its centered disk density is one if $$\frac{N_R}{\pi R^2}\longrightarrow1\qquad(R\to\infty).$$
- **centered-ball energy E_g** ([source](#eq:energy-definition)): For a nonnegative interaction $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$, define $$\label{eq:energy-definition} E_g(\mathcal C)=\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac1{N_R} \sum_{\substack{x,y\in\mathcal C_R\\x\ne y}}g(\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert^2).$$
- **completely monotone interaction** ([source](#stmt-thm-universal)): Let $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth and completely monotone, meaning that $(-1)^r g^{(r)}(t)\ge0$ for every integer $r\ge0$ and every $t>0$.
- **Fourier transform normalization** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We use the normalization $$\widehat f(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb R^2}f(x)e^{-2\pi i x\cdot\xi}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx$$
- **periodic node set** ([source](#eq:node-set)): $$\label{eq:node-set} \begin{gathered} L=\{0,1,3,4,7,9\},\qquad \mathcal Z=12\mathbb Z+L,\\ \mathcal N=\mathcal Z\cap(0,\infty),\qquad \mathcal N_0=\{0\}\cup\mathcal N.
- **sine product P** ([source](#eq:periodic-polynomial)): $$\label{eq:periodic-polynomial} P(s)=\prod_{a\in L}\left(2\sin\frac{\pi(s-a)}{12}\right)^2 =\sum_{l=-6}^{6}P_l e^{i\pi t_l s},\qquad t_l=\frac l6.$$
- **coefficient list and norm** ([source](#eq:cardinal-function)): A coefficient list consists of a real constant $C$ and real pairs $(c_n,d_n)$ for $n\in\mathcal N_0$, with the unweighted norm $$\\|a\\|_1:=\|C\|+\sum_{n\in\mathcal N_0}(\|c_n\|+\|d_n\|)<\infty.$$
- **cardinal function p_a** ([source](#eq:cardinal-function)): Away from the nodes, put $$\label{eq:cardinal-function} p_a(s)=P(s)\left(C+ \sum_{n\in\mathcal N_0} \left(\frac{c_n}{(s-n)^2}+\frac{d_n}{s-n}\right)\right).$$
- **transformed kernel parameters z and lambda** ([source](#eq:transformed-kernel)): For $-1\le t\le1$, set $$\label{eq:transformed-kernel} z(t)=-\frac{B}{t+ih}-ih,\qquad \lambda(t)=\frac{i}{b(t+ih)}.$$
- **transformed function K_{p_a}** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a coefficient list $a$, define $$\label{eq:transformed-function} K_{p_a}(s)=\int_{-1}^1\lambda(t)e^{i\pi z(t)s}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\mu_a(t).$$
- **jet operator S** ([source](#eq:jet-operator)): Put $$\label{eq:jet-operator} Sa=-\mathcal J K_{p_a}.$$
- **slack functions F_i** ([source](#eq:exact-slack-jets)): Write $p_i=p_{\ell_i}$, and combine the two desired inequalities into the slacks $$F_i(s)=\sigma_iH_i(s)+\delta_iT_k(s),\qquad (\sigma_1,\sigma_2)=(-1,1),\qquad \delta_i=\mathbf1_{\{i=1\}}.$$

```json
          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "$$\\label{eq:node-set} \\begin{gathered} L=\\{0,1,3,4,7,9\\},\\qquad \\mathcal Z=12\\mathbb Z+L,\\\\ \\mathcal N=\\mathcal Z\\cap(0,\\infty),\\qquad \\mathcal N_0=\\{0\\}\\cup\\mathcal N.",
      "interpretation": "A periodic superset of all nonzero triangular-lattice shells in the rescaled squared-radius coordinate, where value and slope data are prescribed.",

          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }

      "interpretation": "A nonnegative periodic trigonometric polynomial with a double zero at every node.",

          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }

      "interpretation": "A real constant together with a value coefficient and a slope coefficient at each node, measured by the unweighted sum of absolute values.",

          "source_id": "PM-090.2"
        }

      "exact_definition": "For $-1\\le t\\le1$, set $$\\label{eq:transformed-kernel}\n z(t)=-\\frac{B}{t+ih}-ih,\\qquad\n \\lambda(t)=\\frac{i}{b(t+ih)}.$$",
      "interpretation": "The frequency map and amplitude factor of the Gaussian transform of a damped exponential.",
```

| q-exact-list-norm | norm of the exact lists | < | $5$ | all admissible k |
| q-list-correction | distance of exact lists from reference lists | < | $2\cdot10^{-8}$ | all admissible k |
| q-half-gap-margin | strict quotient margin on half-gaps | > | $.000888$ | all admissible k |
| q-sine-barrier-constant | quadratic barrier constant of the sine product | ≥ | $.68$ | all real s |
| q-tail-second-derivative | second derivative of the tail remainder | < | $.0002$ | all s beyond the junction and all admissible k |
| q-decimal-tolerance | decimal enclosure tolerance | = | $10^{-80}$ | fixed |
| q-exponential-roundoff | error of the rational exponential | < | $10^{-68}$ | complex arguments in the stated domain |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cardinal-measure** [claim](#stmt-lem-cardinal-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cardinal-measure): Integral identities for node fractions, absolute-value variation counts, locally uniform limits, conjugate symmetry. -> entire cardinal function with jets and a finite spectral measure
- **step-frequency-remark** [claim](#stmt-auto-remark-1) / [proof](#stmt-auto-remark-1): Direct substitution in the rational frequency map. -> involution and amplitude identities
- **step-fourier-pair** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-pair): Complex Gaussian transform identity, integration against the spectral measure, double transform equals reflection. -> real radial Schwartz Fourier pair from two lists
- **step-kernel-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-bounds): Explicit monotone kernel estimates and splitting the folded measure at a frequency. -> bounded jet operator and distant-output tail bound
- **step-quadrature-error** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadrature-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadrature-error): Cauchy coefficient estimate on doubled disks plus exactness and positivity of the quadrature. -> uniform moment error bound
- **step-parameter-box** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameter-box) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-box): Affine dependence in each box variable gives a vertex minimum. -> uniform lower bound over a parameter interval
- **step-finite-certificate** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-certificate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-certificate): Doubling power computation and strict ball-arithmetic comparisons of every table entry. -> norm bounds and positive margins
- **step-exact-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-interpolation): Finite-block inverse by Neumann series, Schur complement elimination, residual and correction estimates. -> unique exact lists with double zeros
- **step-exponential-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-exponential-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exponential-support): Monotonicity in the parameter, Taylor tails, Jensen inequality for the mean of the exponential. -> polynomial lower support for the target quotient
- **step-half-gap-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-half-gap-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-half-gap-signs): Bernstein coefficient enclosure combined with list, truncation and support error allowances. -> nonnegative slacks on the bounded range
- **step-sine-barrier** [claim](#stmt-lem-sine-product-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sine-product-barrier): Logarithmic concavity of the divided product and exact midpoint values. -> quadratic lower barrier
- **step-tail-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-signs): Positive low-node rational part against a double-zero remainder with small second derivative. -> nonnegative slacks in the tail
- **step-normalized-pair** [claim](#stmt-thm-normalized-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-signs-2): Assemble exact interpolation, the Fourier pair lemma and the two sign lemmas. -> normalized Gaussian Fourier pair
- **step-density-lp** [claim](#stmt-prop-density-lp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-density-lp): Positive-semidefinite form, Cauchy-Schwarz against a slightly larger disk, dominated convergence. -> density-only linear programming bound
- **step-rational-pi-b** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-pi-b) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-pi-b): Machin identity with truncated arctangent series and integer-square comparisons. -> enclosures of pi and b
- **step-gaussian-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-duality): Rescale the normalized pair for large parameters, Fourier complement for small ones. -> sharp Gaussian minorants
- **step-positive-mixture** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-mixture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-mixture): Finite differences, Bernstein weights, weak compactness preserving endpoint mass, real exponents. -> finite positive representing measure
- **step-energy-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-energy-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-energy-reduction): Shifted mixture, Fatou and Tonelli, monotone convergence in the shift. -> energy lower bound for every completely monotone interaction
- **step-universal** [claim](#stmt-thm-universal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-energy-reduction-2): Combine Gaussian sharp pairs, the density bound and the mixture transfer, then check attainment on the lattice. -> universal energy minimality
- **step-cutoff-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-field-cutoff-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-cutoff-control): Local count estimate, almost monotonicity of cutoff energies, shell averaging. -> cutoff limit and finite infimum
- **step-stationary-minimizers** [claim](#stmt-lem-stationary-field-minimizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-field-minimizers): Orbit averages, compactness, ergodic theorem, two-cutoff identity. -> stationary minimizing law with vanishing defect
- **step-field-screening** [claim](#stmt-lem-field-screening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-screening): Shell of cells with Neumann problems, tree routing of fluxes, rectangle partition for new points. -> screened field without new close pairs
- **step-cubic-approximation** [claim](#stmt-prop-normalized-cubic-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-screening-2): Reflect the screened square, project to the canonical periodic gradient, use the periodic defect identity. -> periodic minimizing sequence
- **step-heat-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison): Heat representation of torus Green functions and dominated convergence. -> integral formula for energy differences
- **step-riesz-jellium** [claim](#stmt-cor-riesz-jellium) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-periodic-heat-comparison-2): Gaussian comparison inside the heat integral, periodic approximation, scalar jellium identification, confinement. -> triangular minimum for renormalized and jellium energies
- **step-rational-roundoff** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-roundoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-roundoff): Stagewise floor-error propagation through exponential, moments, matrix powers and lower evaluations. -> error ceilings for the rational procedure

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-shell-residues**: Every nonzero squared-radius value of the form j^2+j*l+l^2 (the rescaled triangular shell values) falls in one of the residue classes modulo 12 listed for the periodic node set, so it is a positive node.
- **inv-node-superset-strict**: The node set strictly contains the set of shell values: some positive nodes, such as 24, are not represented by the quadratic form.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nodes-are-shells**: Treating every positive node of the periodic set as an actual triangular lattice radius. -> Some nodes are not shell values, so a computation that assumes a lattice point lies on each node would invent points.
- **anti-skip-artificial-nodes**: Dropping the non-shell nodes from an interpolation or certificate computation because they are not lattice radii. -> The construction imposes data at every positive node, so omitting some breaks the stated contacts and the uniqueness setting.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The shell and node description is a finite exact integer construction: for a bound M, enumerate the values of the quadratic form and compare with the periodic residue set. The main analytic theorems are not finite and are not tested here.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer M >= 0 on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: M is a small nonnegative integer (enumeration is quadratic in sqrt(M)).
# OUTPUTS: line 'V:' with sorted positive values j*j+j*l+l*l <= M; line 'X:' with positive nodes <= M (residue mod 12 in 0,1,3,4,7,9) not represented.
# TIME: O(M) arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: O(M) integers.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer arithmetic only.
# FAILURE: malformed or empty input raises an exception (nonzero exit).
import sys
def main():
    M = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
    r = 2 * int(M ** 0.5) + 2
    vals = set()
    for j in range(-r, r + 1):
        for l in range(-r, r + 1):
            q = j * j + j * l + l * l
            if 0 < q <= M:
                vals.add(q)
    nodes = [n for n in range(1, M + 1) if n % 12 in (0, 1, 3, 4, 7, 9)]
    missing = [n for n in nodes if n not in vals]
    print("V: " + " ".join(str(v) for v in sorted(vals)))
    print("X: " + " ".join(str(v) for v in missing))
main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence and sign statements for functions and energies. It specifies no program interface, so no library contract follows from it directly. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The corollary on compatible triangular tori fixes a concrete reference configuration for a neutralized planar particle model with a square number of points. It can serve as a ground-state benchmark for a simulator. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite certificate is a list of strict comparisons on exact arrays, checked by enclosure arithmetic. This is a template for a certified-inequality checker that accepts a comparison only when the whole enclosing ball lies on the correct side of its cutoff. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The quadrature rule on each frequency interval is given by explicit closed formulas for nodes and positive weights, with a proved lower bound on every weight and total mass one. It is a directly codable rule. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No storage, indexing or retrieval structure is proposed or analysed. The arrays of coefficients are computational intermediates, not a data structure result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source gives no hardware design, parallel schedule or cost model. Its matrix computations are described only as exact-array verification. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing here concerns hardness assumptions, protocols or adversaries. The lattice is a planar triangular point set used for an energy comparison. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no learning problem, loss or training procedure. Its positive-mixture representation concerns completely monotone potentials, not models. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The density-only lower bound is an analytic inequality for a fixed class of competitors. It is not posed or solved as a computational optimisation problem with a solver. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus or fault model appears. Locally finite configurations are geometric objects, not networked agents. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A possible further use is formalisation in a proof assistant, but the source supplies no formal development, so it stays outside this contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-completely-monotone** (hypothesis): The universal energy comparison is stated only for smooth interactions of squared distance whose derivatives alternate in sign to every order on the positive half-line. A potential that fails this sign pattern is outside the theorem. Blocked use: ['Applying the triangular-lattice minimality to a potential with sign-changing derivatives, such as an oscillatory or Lennard-Jones-type interaction']
- **limit-centered-density-one** (hypothesis): The linear-programming lower bound and hence the energy comparison use the centered disk density hypothesis as the only geometric input. A configuration whose centered disk count does not grow like the disk area is not covered. Blocked use: ['Comparing a configuration of different density, or one that is not locally finite, with the unit-covolume lattice without rescaling and checking the hypothesis']
- **limit-schwartz-fourier-positivity** (hypothesis): The density bound needs a real even Schwartz function with nonnegative Fourier transform. Functions without Fourier positivity give no lower bound by this argument. Blocked use: ['Using an arbitrary test function without verifying that its transform is nonnegative']
- **limit-gaussian-parameter-range** (hypothesis): The normalized Gaussian pair is asserted for finite real parameters at or above the stated threshold. Below it the sign and interpolation certificates do not apply, and small Gaussian scales are handled separately by Fourier complementation. Blocked use: ['Reusing the normalized pair directly for parameters below the threshold', 'Extending the certificate tables to parameter values they do not cover']
- **limit-finite-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): The finite certificate consists of strict comparisons on exact arrays whose enclosure is carried out by ball arithmetic. Its conclusion is tied to the specific block sizes, quadrature rule and parameter partition of the construction, and is not a machine-checked formal proof. Blocked use: ['Citing the matrix and sign bounds as formally verified', 'Changing the block size or partition and keeping the printed cutoffs']
- **limit-rational-procedure-conditional** (approximation): The alternative floor-rounded rational procedure only transfers its tests to the looser exact-array bounds if every test passes and the stated norm envelopes hold. Its roundoff allowances are conditional on these envelopes. Blocked use: ['Treating the rational procedure as an unconditional proof of the finite certificate', 'Using it with exponential arguments outside the stated domain']
- **limit-riesz-exponent-range** (edge_case): The renormalized and jellium minimality is proved for exponents from zero up to but excluding two, on compatible triangular tori. The boundary exponent, quantum crystallization, uniqueness and defect exclusion are explicitly not claimed. Blocked use: ['Applying the corollary at the excluded exponent', 'Concluding uniqueness of the minimizer or absence of defects', 'Using the torus statement for incompatible torus shapes or non-square point counts']
- **limit-per-potential-auxiliary** (formal_scope): Sharp auxiliary functions are constructed only for Gaussians. Integrating the Gaussian inequalities gives the energy comparison for mixtures, but no sharp auxiliary function is produced for each mixed potential. Blocked use: ['Expecting a closed-form sharp Fourier auxiliary for an arbitrary completely monotone potential from this construction']
- **limit-positive-mixture-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The representing measure of a completely monotone function is obtained as a weak limit along a subsequence, so it is an existence statement with no finite procedure for computing the measure. Blocked use: ['Presenting the mixture measure as an explicitly computable object', 'Deriving a complexity bound for obtaining it']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove universal energy minimality for the triangular lattice in the plane. Among locally finite configurations of centered density one, it minimizes the lower limit of centered-ball energy averages for every nonnegative completely monotone function of squared distance, including when the energy is infinite. We also prove triangular minimality for planar logarithmic and Riesz renormalized energies, with $`0\lt s\lt 2`$ in the Riesz case, and the corresponding jellium minima. The proof uses sharp Gaussian Fourier bounds, positive mixtures, and heat-kernel comparison.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Universal optimality asks whether one configuration minimizes an entire class of pair interactions. In the plane, the triangular lattice is the natural candidate. The problem goes beyond comparison with other lattices: a competing configuration may be nonperiodic, may contain arbitrarily close pairs, and may have very uneven local point counts. We prove the energy comparison for this full class under a single centered-density hypothesis.

<a id="eq:energy-definition"></a>
Put $b=\sqrt3/2$ and let $$A=b^{-1/2}\{j(1,0)+k(1/2,b):j,k\in\mathbb Z\}$$ be the triangular lattice of covolume one. Let $B_R$ be the closed disk of radius $R$ centered at the origin. For a locally finite configuration $\mathcal C\subset\mathbb R^2$, write $\mathcal C_R=\mathcal C\cap B_R$ and $N_R=\#\mathcal C_R$. Its centered disk density is one if $$\frac{N_R}{\pi R^2}\longrightarrow1\qquad(R\to\infty).$$ For a nonnegative interaction $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$, define $$\label{eq:energy-definition}
 E_g(\mathcal C)=\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac1{N_R}
 \sum_{\substack{x,y\in\mathcal C_R\\x\ne y}}g(\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert^2).$$ The sum counts ordered pairs. Density one makes $N_R$ positive for all sufficiently large $R$, so the lower limit is well defined in $[0,\infty]$.

<a id="stmt-thm-universal"></a>
<div id="thm:universal" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Universal energy minimality). *Let $g:(0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth and completely monotone, meaning that $(-1)^r g^{(r)}(t)\ge0$ for every integer $r\ge0$ and every $t>0$. For every locally finite configuration $\mathcal C\subset\mathbb R^2$ of centered disk density one, the following equality and inequality hold in the extended nonnegative reals: <a id="eq:main-inequality"></a>$$\label{eq:main-inequality}
 E_g(\mathcal C)\ge
 \sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}g(\left\lvert a\right\rvert^2)=E_g(A).$$*

</div>

This includes every Gaussian $g(t)=e^{-\pi\alpha t}$ with $\alpha>0$ and every inverse power $g(t)=t^{-p}$ with $p>0$. The theorem permits singular potentials and divergent lattice sums, and it imposes no separation or local occupancy bound on $\mathcal C$. It identifies the minimum value, not all minimizers. The comparison uses the centered-density and lower-energy formulation of Cohn et al. (Cohn et al. 2022, Definitions 1.1–1.3) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[CKMRV2022](#ref-CKMRV2022)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The planar problem and its predecessors

The lattice problem began with inverse-power interactions. For the convergent Epstein zeta sums, Rankin (Rankin 1953) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Rankin1953](#ref-Rankin1953)] proved the triangular minimum in a restricted exponent range, and Cassels (Cassels 1959) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Cassels1959](#ref-Cassels1959)] addressed the remaining range. Cassels subsequently acknowledged a gap in his proof (Cassels 1963) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Cassels1963Corrigendum](#ref-Cassels1963Corrigendum)]; Ennola (Ennola 1964) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Ennola1964Lemma](#ref-Ennola1964Lemma)] supplied a repair, and Diananda (Diananda 1964) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Diananda1964](#ref-Diananda1964)] simplified the needed lemmas. Together these works establish the triangular minimum among planar lattices of fixed covolume for $g(t)=t^{-p}$ with $p>1$. Montgomery’s theta theorem gives the Gaussian minimum for every positive parameter (Montgomery 1988, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Montgomery1988](#ref-Montgomery1988)]. These results identify the best lattice; they leave comparison with nonlattice configurations open.

Cohn and Kumar placed this question alongside their study of universal energy minimization on spheres (Cohn and Kumar 2007, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CohnKumar2007](#ref-CohnKumar2007)]. Their Euclidean conjecture singled out the triangular, $E_8$, and Leech lattices (Cohn and Kumar 2007, Conjecture 9.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CohnKumar2007](#ref-CohnKumar2007)]. It asks for a sharp auxiliary function for each sufficiently decaying completely monotone squared-distance interaction: a Fourier lower bound that attains the lattice energy and therefore proves the comparison for periodic competitors. In dimensions $8$ and $24$, Cohn, Kumar, Miller, Radchenko, and Viazovska proved universal optimality and constructed sharp Gaussian bounds and Fourier interpolation formulas (Cohn et al. 2022, Theorems 1.4, 1.7, and 1.9) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CKMRV2022](#ref-CKMRV2022)].

Several planar results address restricted competitor classes. Faulhuber et al. (Faulhuber, Shafkulovska, and Zlotnikov 2024, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[FaulhuberShafkulovskaZlotnikov2023](#ref-FaulhuberShafkulovskaZlotnikov2023)] compare the triangular lattice with the honeycomb configuration, Cartesian products of one-dimensional periodic configurations, and unions of two translates of a rectangular or triangular lattice, with each class normalized to the same density. Hardin and Tenpas (Hardin and Tenpas 2025, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HardinTenpas2025](#ref-HardinTenpas2025)] prove triangular optimality among four-point configurations modulo a fixed triangular lattice and six-point configurations modulo $\mathbb Z\times\sqrt3\mathbb Z$, with the appropriate scaling and rotation of the minimizing lattice. Their completely monotone interactions satisfy a rapid-decay assumption. Leblé (Leblé 2025, Theorems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Leble2025](#ref-Leble2025)] proves that sufficiently small bounded displacements of the triangular lattice cannot lower the energy; the permitted displacement size depends on the interaction. These restrictions are different from the centered-density hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">1</a>.

Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">1</a> proves the positive planar universal-energy conclusion in the formulation stated above. The construction also supplies sharp auxiliary functions for every Gaussian. Integrating the resulting energy inequalities does not construct a sharp auxiliary for each mixed potential in the class required by Cohn and Kumar’s conjecture. That stronger per-potential quantifier is not claimed here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The Gaussian route and the interpolation problem

The proof reduces the energy problem to a family of Fourier inequalities. We use the normalization $$\widehat f(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb R^2}f(x)e^{-2\pi i x\cdot\xi}\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm dx$$ and write $A^*=\{w\in\mathbb R^2:w\cdot a\in\mathbb Z\text{ for every }a\in A\}$ for the dual lattice. Fix a Gaussian $G_\alpha(x)=e^{-\pi\alpha|x|^2}$, with $\alpha>0$. We seek a real radial Schwartz function $f_\alpha$ satisfying $$f_\alpha\le G_\alpha,\qquad \widehat f_\alpha\ge0,
 \qquad
 \begin{aligned}
 f_\alpha(a)&=G_\alpha(a)&& (a\in A\setminus\{0\}),\\
 \widehat f_\alpha(w)&=0&& (w\in A^*\setminus\{0\}).
 \end{aligned}$$ The two inequalities give a lower bound for arbitrary competitors. The contacts at the direct and dual lattice points make the bound exact on $A$. For every competitor $\mathcal C$ of centered disk density one, Section <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">6</a> proves $$E_{e^{-\pi\alpha t}}(\mathcal C)
 \ge\widehat f_\alpha(0)-f_\alpha(0)
 =\sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}e^{-\pi\alpha|a|^2}.$$ The lower bound compares the point measure in a disk with area measure in a slightly larger disk. Its error is uniform even when points cluster. The equality follows from Poisson summation on $A$; the competitor itself need not be periodic.

Exact contacts and signs between contacts are different requirements. The first is an infinite interpolation problem. The second must hold at every radius and every Gaussian scale. Our construction separates them, then uses a quantitative correction estimate to bring them together. The normalized auxiliary theorem, Theorem <a href="#thm:normalized-pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:normalized-pair">2</a>, is stated after the shell and node definitions in Section <a href="#sec:fourier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fourier">2</a>. Its periodic set of rescaled squared radii contains all triangular-lattice shells, so the prescribed values and first derivatives (jets) supply the required contacts.

The construction has three stages. First, Section <a href="#sec:fourier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fourier">2</a> encodes the periodic contact set by a sine product with double zeros. Multiplying that product by summable double-pole and simple-pole terms gives entire functions with independently specified values and slopes at the nodes. A compactly supported spectral measure represents each such cardinal function. Gaussian damping turns it into a radial Schwartz function, and transforming the Gaussian inside the integral gives its genuine Fourier transform. Combining two cardinal functions with their transformed profiles reduces the simultaneous contacts to coupled linear equations for two summable coefficient lists. The transformed jets become uniformly small at distant output nodes, regardless of the input coefficient locations.

Second, Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">3</a> constructs finite reference lists by positive quadrature, leaving the spectral atoms exact. It proves a uniform quadrature error and certifies explicit matrix and polynomial inequalities. These lists do not yet interpolate exactly. The available bound on the full coupled operator is also too large for a direct small-perturbation argument. Section <a href="#sec:interpolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:interpolation">4</a> instead inverts the finite block and eliminates it; the small distant outputs make the remaining tail operator invertible. This solves all the exact interpolation equations and bounds the correction to the reference lists.

Third, Section <a href="#sec:signs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signs">5</a> proves the signs corresponding to $G_\alpha-f_\alpha\ge0$ and $\widehat f_\alpha\ge0$. It works in the rescaled squared-radius coordinate, after removing positive Gaussian factors. The normalized difference and Fourier profile then vanish to second order at the positive nodes because of the exact value-and-slope contacts. To control them near a node, we subtract the value and linear term of each component, divide by the squared distance to the node, and then approximate the quotient by a polynomial. This keeps a small interpolation error from being divided by a vanishing square. Coefficients in the Bernstein basis bound the polynomial on whole intervals between nodes, and rectangular bounds on its parameter data cover whole parameter intervals. On the unbounded radial tail, the signed contribution from the low-node rational terms is positive. The sum of the remaining terms has double zeros and a small second derivative. A quadratic lower bound for the sine product puts the positive part and the remainder on the same scale. Appendix <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">9</a> supplies the scalar bounds used in this argument and describes the accompanying interval verifier for the exact quadrature arrays. Appendix <a href="#app:floor-certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:floor-certificate">11</a> gives a separate rational verification procedure, whose implication is conditional on its tests passing.

Section <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">6</a> converts the normalized construction into the functions $f_\alpha$ above. It directly covers $\alpha\ge1$; a Fourier-complement construction supplies $0<\alpha<1$. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:mixtures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mixtures">7</a> represents each positive shift of a completely monotone potential as $$g(\varepsilon+t)=\int_{[0,1]}v^t\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm d\rho_\varepsilon(v)
 \qquad(t>0),$$ where $\varepsilon>0$ and $\rho_\varepsilon$ is positive with total mass $g(\varepsilon)$. The shift makes this mass finite even when $g$ is singular at zero. For $0<v<1$, the kernel $v^t$ is a Gaussian with $\alpha=-\log(v)/\pi$; the endpoint $v=1$ gives the constant interaction. Fatou’s lemma and Tonelli’s theorem transfer the Gaussian comparisons to the mixture, and the shift is removed only in the lattice sum. This proves Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">1</a>, including singular potentials and infinite energies.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Antecedents of the method

The interpolation mechanism has substantial antecedents in the Beurling–Selberg extremal method. Cardinal formulas with squared trigonometric factors, double-pole value terms, and simple-pole derivative terms occur in the one-dimensional construction of Cohn and Kumar (Cohn and Kumar 2007, Proposition 9.6) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CohnKumar2007](#ref-CohnKumar2007)] and in the Gaussian extremal functions of Carneiro et al. (Carneiro, Littmann, and Vaaler 2013, secs. 1, (1.2)–(1.3)) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CarneiroLittmannVaaler2013](#ref-CarneiroLittmannVaaler2013)]. Radchenko and Viazovska (Radchenko and Viazovska 2019, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[RadchenkoViazovska2019](#ref-RadchenkoViazovska2019)] reconstruct even Schwartz functions on the real line from simultaneous function and Fourier data at square-root nodes. Theorems 1.7 and 1.9 of Cohn et al. (Cohn et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CKMRV2022](#ref-CKMRV2022)] give radial value-and-derivative reconstruction formulas in dimensions $8$ and $24$. In the plane, simultaneous values and first derivatives of a radial Schwartz function and its Fourier transform on the triangular shells do not determine the function: Talebizadeh Sardari (Talebizadeh Sardari 2021, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Sardari2021](#ref-Sardari2021)] proves that infinitely many linearly independent functions have all these data zero. His proof uses periodic congruence supersets of the triangular quadratic-form values (Talebizadeh Sardari 2021, sec. 6.1, Lemma 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Sardari2021](#ref-Sardari2021)]. Our construction prescribes data on a periodic superset and proves uniqueness within its specified summable coefficient system. The following sections establish exact interpolation in that system and the parameter-uniform planar signs.

Related Fourier sign conditions enter the sphere-packing bound of Cohn and Elkies (Cohn and Elkies 2003) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[CohnElkies2003](#ref-CohnElkies2003)]. The companion manuscript (OpenAI 2026a, secs. 2–4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[SharpPlanar2026](#ref-SharpPlanar2026)] develops the cardinal, Gaussian-transform, and finite sign-certification framework for a sharp planar packing bound. We adapt that framework to a continuously varying Gaussian target and establish the required energy identities, correction estimates, and signs locally. The separate atomic companion independently proves the same energy conclusion (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[AtomicTriangular2026](#ref-AtomicTriangular2026)]; this is not a premise of the present proof. Its auxiliary construction is compared with ours after Theorem <a href="#thm:normalized-pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:normalized-pair">2</a>.

The density-only linear-programming argument follows Cohn and de Courcy-Ireland (Cohn and Courcy-Ireland 2018, Proposition 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[CohnDeCourcyIreland2018](#ref-CohnDeCourcyIreland2018)], whose background-measure method follows Cohn and Zhao (Cohn and Zhao 2014, Theorem 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CohnZhao2014](#ref-CohnZhao2014)]. The latter is a packing theorem; we use the method, not its exclusion-distance hypotheses. Section <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">6</a> proves the needed statement for real even Schwartz functions with nonnegative Fourier transform. The positive-mixture representation belongs to the classical Hausdorff–Bernstein–Widder theory (Hausdorff 1921; Bernstein 1929; Widder 1931) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Hausdorff1921](#ref-Hausdorff1921), [Bernstein1929](#ref-Bernstein1929), [Widder1931](#ref-Widder1931)]; our finite-difference and moment-measure proof is given in Section <a href="#sec:mixtures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mixtures">7</a>. The lower-limit, endpoint, and infinite-energy arguments are proved there as well.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Renormalized and jellium consequences

For $0<s<2$, the raw Riesz energy with $g(t)=t^{-s/2}$ is infinite even on $A$, so the comparison of raw energies does not by itself distinguish configurations in this range. Neutralized energies account for a uniform oppositely charged background. In a finite jellium system, one combines point–point, point–background, and background–background interactions; the thermodynamic jellium minimum is the large-volume limit of the minimum energy per unit volume. Renormalized energies remove the divergent self-energy of each point. Their periodic version compares configurations in a fixed torus, and their infinite-system version measures field energy per unit area. Petrache and Serfaty (Petrache and Serfaty 2020, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[PetracheSerfaty2020](#ref-PetracheSerfaty2020)] proved that the Cohn–Kumar universal-optimality conjecture implies the corresponding Coulomb and Riesz crystallization results. Their heat-kernel representation (Petrache and Serfaty 2020, Lemma 1) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[PetracheSerfaty2020](#ref-PetracheSerfaty2020)] supplies the connection to Gaussian comparisons used here. We carry out this passage for the field functional defined in Section <a href="#sec:renormalized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:renormalized">8</a>, including its normalization and periodic approximation, after completing the main theorem in Section <a href="#sec:mixtures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mixtures">7</a>.

Corollary <a href="#cor:riesz-jellium" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:riesz-jellium">19</a> proves the triangular minimum for every $0\le s<2$, with interaction $-\log|x|$ at $s=0$ and $|x|^{-s}$ for $0<s<2$. On each torus $\mathbb R^2/(nA)$, the $n^2$ classes of $A/(nA)$ minimize the canonical periodic energy among all configurations of $n^2$ distinct points. The lattice also minimizes the infinite-system field energy $W_s$ among density-one configurations, and the ordered-pair thermodynamic jellium minimum is $W_s(A)/\kappa_s$, where $\kappa_0=2\pi$ and $\kappa_s=4\pi$ for $0<s<2$.

Section <a href="#sec:renormalized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:renormalized">8</a> defines these quantities with unit background and fixes the field normalization by the fundamental-solution equation. The field energy takes the centered-square upper limit at each fixed self-energy cutoff, then removes the cutoff; the infimum over compatible gradient fields is outside both limits. The proof compares heat-kernel energies for arbitrary period lattices. Appendix <a href="#app:normalized-periodic-approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:normalized-periodic-approximation">10</a> constructs a sequence of square-periodic fields approaching the field infimum, so the comparison does not require a periodic approximation with prescribed triangular periods. The scalar periodic and jellium identities of Lewin et al. (Lewin, Lieb, and Seiringer 2019, sec. VI, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[LewinLiebSeiringer2019](#ref-LewinLiebSeiringer2019)] and Lauritsen (Lauritsen 2021, Theorem II.1) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Lauritsen2021](#ref-Lauritsen2021)] then identify the thermodynamic value.

The fresh long-range conclusion here is $0<s<2$. At $s=0$, the corollary overlaps the independent direct logarithmic result of the Coulomb companion (OpenAI 2026c, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[CoulombRenormalized2026](#ref-CoulombRenormalized2026)], which uses its own field-energy normalization and proves a lower bound on square tori. The periodic assertion above concerns only the compatible triangular tori $\mathbb R^2/(nA)$. It asserts neither uniqueness nor exclusion of defects, and makes no claim at $s=2$ or for quantum crystallization.

<a id="sec:fourier"></a>
### Cardinal interpolation and Fourier transformation

The auxiliary pair will be assembled from two summable lists of interpolation data. We first realize each list by an entire function whose values and first derivatives are determined by its coordinates. Gaussian damping then turns this function into a radial Schwartz function, and Fourier transformation gives a second kernel with rapidly decreasing jets at distant nodes. The estimates in this section apply to arbitrary summable lists, before any finite approximation is introduced.

The spectral construction adapts the cardinal-measure and Gaussian-transform method of OpenAI (OpenAI 2026a, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[SharpPlanar2026](#ref-SharpPlanar2026)]. The formulas and estimates needed for the present target are proved below; no packing sign bound is used.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Shells and interpolation nodes

Set the construction parameters and radial coordinate as follows: <a id="eq:normalization"></a>$$\label{eq:normalization}
 b=\frac{\sqrt3}{2},\qquad h=\frac25,\qquad
 B=\frac43=\frac1{b^2},\qquad s=b|x|^2.$$ The same radial coordinate is used on the Fourier side. For the unit-covolume triangular lattice $A$, take the basis $$u=\sqrt{2/\sqrt3}\,(1,0),\qquad
 v=\sqrt{2/\sqrt3}\,(1/2,\sqrt3/2).$$ Then $$b|ju+\ell v|^2=j^2+j\ell+\ell^2\qquad(j,\ell\in\mathbb Z).$$ Modulo 3 the right side equals $(j-\ell)^2$, and is therefore 0 or 1. Modulo 2 it is even only when both $j$ and $\ell$ are even, in which case it is divisible by 4. These two restrictions give the residue classes listed below. Thus every nonzero shell belongs to the positive part of the following periodic set: <a id="eq:node-set"></a>$$\label{eq:node-set}
 \begin{gathered}
 L=\{0,1,3,4,7,9\},\qquad \mathcal Z=12\mathbb Z+L,\\
 \mathcal N=\mathcal Z\cap(0,\infty),\qquad
 \mathcal N_0=\{0\}\cup\mathcal N.
 \end{gathered}$$ The inclusion is strict. For example, $24\in\mathcal N$, but if $j^2+j\ell+\ell^2=24$, the parity implication makes $j,\ell$ even. Dividing by four gives the same quadratic form with value 6, so its two arguments are again even. The original value would then be divisible by 16, contrary to $16\nmid24$. Such additional nodes will also carry prescribed interpolation data.

Let $J$ denote rotation through a positive right angle. Since $\det(u,v)=1$, the vectors $-Jv,Ju$ are dual to $u,v$. They generate $JA$, because $-v,u$ form another basis of $A$. Hence $A^*=JA$, so the direct and dual lattices have the same radial shells.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The normalized Gaussian theorem

The Fourier transform uses the convention fixed in the introduction. The common direct and dual shell set allows us to impose contacts at every positive node of $\mathcal N$. The following theorem is the auxiliary result proved in Sections <a href="#sec:fourier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fourier">2</a>–<a href="#sec:signs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signs">5</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-normalized-pair"></a>
<div id="thm:normalized-pair" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:normalized-jets"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Normalized Gaussian Fourier pair). *For every finite real $k\ge2.36$, put $T_k(s)=k^{-1}e^{-k(s-1)}$. There are entire functions $H_1,H_2$, real on the real axis, and a real radial Schwartz function $f$ on $\mathbb R^2$ such that $$f(x)=e^{-\pi h b\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2}H_1(b\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2),\qquad
 \widehat f(\xi)=e^{-\pi h b\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^2}H_2(b\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^2).$$ For every real $s\ge0$, <a id="eq:normalized-signs"></a>$$\label{eq:normalized-signs}
 H_1(s)\le T_k(s),\qquad H_2(s)\ge0.$$ At every $n\in\mathcal N$, the values and first derivatives satisfy $$\label{eq:normalized-jets}
 \begin{gathered}
 H_1(n)=T_k(n),\qquad H_1'(n)=T_k'(n)=-e^{-k(n-1)},\\
 H_2(n)=H_2'(n)=0.
 \end{gathered}$$*

</div>

The full parameter range $k\ge2.36$ and every artificial node are part of the assertion, beyond the contacts needed for the energy inequalities. The theorem asserts existence; uniqueness will be proved only in the fixed coefficient system of Proposition <a href="#prop:exact-interpolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exact-interpolation">10</a>.

For $\alpha\ge1$, multiplying the pair by $ke^{-k}$ with $k=\pi(\alpha/b-h)$ gives a minorant of $e^{-\pi\alpha\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2}$. A Fourier-complement construction supplies $0<\alpha<1$. Section <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">6</a> proves this conversion and the sharp Gaussian energy comparison; Section <a href="#sec:mixtures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mixtures">7</a> then proves Theorem <a href="#thm:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:universal">1</a>.

The atomic companion (OpenAI 2026b, secs. 3–4) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[AtomicTriangular2026](#ref-AtomicTriangular2026)] uses finite atomic columns, a prescribed node set omitting $24$, and Gaussian damping $h=17/50$.
